# Estate Consolidation Standard (ECS), edition for period 2026Q3

This standard defines how the storage team consolidates the file inventories of every estate volume into one
placement manifest per deployment profile. It is the authority for every rule below; where an export, a draft or a
wiki page disagrees with this standard, this standard wins. Section numbers are cited by the other documents.

## 1. Inputs and outputs

All inputs are the files listed in Appendix R of source-register.md. Volume exports (inventories) and change journals
are the record sources; every other file is a reference table. Each file carries a control record (section 10.4).
The consolidation is run once per profile, `prod` and `dev`, with that profile's effective configuration.

## 2. Configuration

### 2.1 Layers

The effective configuration is built from four layers, applied in this order:
layer 10 `estate-defaults.toml`, layer 20 `site-policy.yaml`, layer 30 `volume-policy.json`, layer 40
`operator-overrides.ini`. A later layer replaces a key set by an earlier layer. Inside one layer, rows whose profile is
`*` apply first and rows for the profile being resolved apply after them (and win). Rows for any other profile are ignored.

### 2.2 Locks, tickets, spellings

A row with final=true locks its key for every later layer (a later layer may not change it); the lock does not stop a
profile row of the same layer. Layer 40 rows apply only when their ticket is approved (2.4) and their override_id has not
been revoked. Revocations are published only in the revocations list of site-policy-v2.yaml; a revocation takes effect
when its ticket is approved. That file is a proposal and none of its settings are in force.
Values are typed by key: window.start and window.end are ISO-8601 UTC strings, purge.enabled and subtree flags are
booleans, store.block_size is an integer number of bytes, vol.<VOLUME>.zone and vol.<VOLUME>.case are strings.
Booleans may be written true/false, yes/no, on/off or 1/0 in any letter case. Integers may carry `_` or `,` grouping and,
in the ini layer, a KiB or MiB suffix (64 KiB = 65536). The key namespace before the first dot is case-insensitive and
is written in lower case in outputs; the volume code is upper case; the path part of a subtree key is kept as written.
Subtree keys have the form subtree.<VOLUME>:<canonical prefix>.<flag> with flag purge_exempt or archive_exempt.

### 2.3 Text normalisation

Before any key or code is compared: remove U+FEFF and U+200B, trim surrounding spaces, and normalise to Unicode NFC.
Text files may start with a UTF-8 byte order mark that is not part of the first value. Identifiers (record ids, ticket ids,
hold ids, batch ids, serials, override ids) compare case-insensitively and are written in upper case in outputs.
Retention class names, zone ids and principal ids also compare case-insensitively; classes are written in lower case.

### 2.4 Tickets

change_tickets.csv lists ticket revisions. The state of a ticket is the state of its highest rev. A ticket is approved
when that state is APPROVED and its decided_utc is strictly before window.end. decided_utc 0000-00-00T00:00:00Z means
the decision was never recorded: the ticket is not approved.

## 3. Authority

### 3.1 Exports

Every inventory declares its own run_mode in its header or metadata. Only run_mode full and continuation are
authoritative. Exports declaring any other mode (dryrun, deep-scan, ltfs-shadow) are not authoritative and every record
they carry is OUT_OF_AUTHORITY, except inside an approved scope override (3.2). The newest export of a volume is not
automatically the authoritative one.

### 3.2 Scope overrides

The scope sheet of authority_matrix.xlsx lists records of type override: (id, subject file, volume, prefix, ticket).
With an approved ticket, the subject file is authoritative for that volume below the canonical prefix (whole path
segments), and records of every other export of that volume below the prefix become SUPERSEDED_SCOPE.

### 3.3 Snapshots and journals

The snapshot instant of a volume is completed_utc of its runs-sheet row whose kind is full, status complete and
superseded_by empty, for the volume's full export. Journals declaring role primary are authoritative. A replica journal
is authoritative only for the sequence range named in ops-runbook.md; its other events are OUT_OF_AUTHORITY.

### 3.4 Voids

Void registers: void_register_q3.csv (A) and void_register_supplement.jsonl (B). Per record id, the row with the
highest rev across both decides; the record is VOIDED when that row has status active and an approved ticket.
Reinstatements are published only as reinstate elements of void_register_q3_r2.xml (a draft whose void rows are not in
force); an approved reinstatement cancels the void. Exclusions in audit-scope-letter-fin.pdf also void (reason VOIDED).

### 3.5 Errata

Errata registers: errata_register_q3.csv (A), errata_register_tape.xml (B), errata-bulletin-q3.docx (C). Per record and
field, the row with the highest rev among rows with an approved ticket that are not withdrawn decides. Withdrawals are
published only in errata_register_q3_r2.tsv (rows with field WITHDRAW) and take effect with an approved ticket; that
file's other rows are a preview and not in force. Fields: bytes, mtime, sha256, owner. An erratum replaces the resolved
value of that field (so an unknown size can become known); it never changes authority, voids or exclusions.

## 4. Time

### 4.1 Output form

Every instant is written YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ (UTC, whole seconds).

### 4.2 Dialects

Each source writes times in its dialect (Appendix S of source-register.md). filetime counts 100 ns ticks since
1601-01-01 UTC; epoch_s, epoch_ms, epoch_ns and epoch_frac count from 1970-01-01 UTC. Sub-second parts are floored toward
the past (never rounded up), also for instants before 1970. iso_z, iso_ms, iso_offset and iso_mixed are ISO-8601 with Z
or an explicit offset; rfc2822 is an e-mail Date header. julian is YYYYDDD HHMMSS in UTC (DDD is the day of the year).
Excel serials: a workbook with date1904=1 counts days since 1904-01-01 (this workbook family writes UTC); otherwise days
since 1899-12-30 (serials 0 and 60 mean not captured). The fractional day is converted to seconds and rounded half-even
to the nearest second. When a time is not captured, the record's batch captured_utc is used.

### 4.3 Local time

Dialects local, local_dmy, local_tzq and excel1900_local are wall-clock times of the volume's zone: the vol.<V>.zone
setting when present, else the zone of the volume's host (volumes.yaml) in the host registers (Appendix H of
source-register.md, part A; ECS-annex-hosts-rev6.pdf, part B). Zone offsets come only from tz_transitions_a.tsv and
tz_transitions_b.parquet: segments are half-open [from_utc, to_utc); offset_seconds may be written as seconds or +hh:mm.
A wall-clock time that maps to exactly one segment is read with that offset. A time that maps to two (fall-back overlap)
is read with the smaller offset (standard time). A time that maps to none (spring-forward gap) is read with the zone's
standard offset (the offset of its is_dst=0 segments). local_tzq carries tzq, the offset in quarter hours; when tzq is
null the zone rule applies.

### 4.4 Journal clock incidents

Adopted clock incidents (Appendix D of ECS-annex-hosts-rev7.pdf) correct journal events that carry a host (section 8).

## 5. Paths and owners

### 5.1 Raw forms

Raw paths are UNC (\\server\share\...), smb://, nfs://, posix roots (/exports/..., /ltfs/..., /mnt/...), or
s3://bucket/key. Only raw paths that begin with two backslashes have their backslashes turned into forward slashes;
posix names may legitimately contain backslashes.

### 5.2 Alias roots

Alias tables: A alias_remap_nas.tsv, B alias_remap_sharepoint.xlsx, C alias_remap_dfs.xml, D ECS-handbook-paths.docx,
E volumes.yaml, F Appendix F of this standard. Each row maps a root (ending with a separator) to a volume and a
canonical prefix. UNC, smb and nfs roots compare case-insensitively; posix roots compare case-sensitively. For one root
the row with the highest rev decides; when that row is retired the root resolves nothing and the path is
PATH_UNRESOLVED (there is no fallback to a shorter root). A raw path resolves through the longest root that is a prefix of
it ending at a separator. The volume column holds a volume code or serial:<SERIAL>; serials resolve through the serials
list of volumes_cmdb.json (per serial the highest rev decides), and only active serials resolve.

### 5.3 Canonical form

The remainder after the root is split on /; empty and . segments are dropped; .. removes the previous segment (a ..
above the root makes the path unresolved); each segment is normalised to NFC. Canonical path = prefix + segments, no
trailing slash. Case: ntfs, hfsplus and exfat volumes are case-insensitive, other volumes case-sensitive, and apfs uses
the vol.<V>.case setting. On a case-insensitive volume the canonical path is lowered with simple Unicode lower-casing
(Python str.lower; never full case folding, so straße stays straße and ẞ becomes ß). Case-sensitive subtrees on
case-insensitive volumes are listed in case_sensitive_dirs.csv (ENG02) and case_sensitive_dirs_leg01.xml (LEG01): per
prefix (compared in lower case) the highest rev decides and only active rows apply; when the lowered path starts with a
listed prefix, the path is lowered up to and including that prefix and kept as written below it. OBJ01 keys: the
canonical path is /<bucket>/<key> with %XX sequences decoded as UTF-8 bytes; + is a literal plus.

### 5.4 Owner tokens

Namespaces and their maps: SIDs (sids sheet of principal_sid_map.xlsx; SID history from the sid_history sheet of
principal_directory.xlsx, highest rev per old SID, is applied first); uid:<host>:<number> (principal_uid_map.tsv; the
host may be an alias listed in host_aliases.ini; numbers compare as integers); CORP\login (owner-migration-plan.docx);
OLDCORP\login (the OLDCORP table of principal_map_ldap.html); other DOMAIN\login (principal_legacy.db); e-mail
(hr-roster-q3.pdf, lower case, +tag ignored); S3 canonical ids (principal_s3_ids.json, lower-case hex); svc- accounts
(service_accounts.ini, lower case); MF ids (principal_mainframe_ids.txt, upper case). SIDs compare in upper case;
DOMAIN\login compares the domain in upper case and the login in lower case; host names compare in lower case. Only rows
with status active are used; when a token appears more than once the row with the latest valid_from wins. The principal
id must exist in the principals sheet of principal_sid_map.xlsx (the principal register), else the owner is unresolved.
The sentinel tokens of Appendix S mean the owner was not captured.

### 5.5 Merges

principal_merges.csv and Appendix M of ops-runbook.md list merges. Per from_principal the highest rev decides; a merge
applies when its status is applied and effective_utc is strictly before window.end. Follow merges until none applies.
The output owner is the account of the final principal in the principal register.

## 6. Sizes and content

### 6.1 Sizes

Sizes are bytes unless the dialect says otherwise. Sources with size dialect unit write the size in the unit of the
record's batch: batch registers export_batches_a.csv (A), export_batches_b.tsv (B), tape-operations-notes.docx (C) and
units-bulletin.pdf (D); bulletin rows with source=correction override the unit of that batch elsewhere. Units: B, KB
(1000), KiB (1024), MiB (1048576), blk512, blk4096. Converted sizes are exact: use decimal arithmetic, never binary
floats. kib is KiB as an exact decimal; -0 is zero. kb4de is KB with up to four decimals in de-DE notation
(1.234,5675) and is converted to bytes rounding half-even. Grouping separators (comma, dot, space, apostrophe, thin or
narrow space) may appear in integer sizes. A size given as the source's unknown-size sentinel is SIZE_UNKNOWN.

### 6.2 Digests

A record's sha256 column wins when present. Otherwise its legacy digest is mapped through the crosswalk partition of the
record's volume: md5A hash_xwalk_md5_eng.csv (ENG02, LEG01), md5B hash_xwalk_md5_mkt.jsonl (MKT01, OBJ01, LAB01), md5C
hash_xwalk_md5_fin.parquet (HR01), sha1A hash_xwalk_sha1_fin.json (FIN02), sha1B vendor-hash-attestation.pdf (RND01),
and for ARC01 the rows of hash_index_master.parquet whose origin is ARC01 (its other rows are reference copies). Digests
compare in lower-case hex; per digest the highest rev decides and only status ok maps. MKT01 writes MD5 in base64; S3
ETags lose their quotes and multipart ETags (digest-N) are looked up whole. An all-zero digest means not computed; an
unmapped digest is HASH_UNRESOLVED.

### 6.3 Extreme values

Zero-byte files, multi-terabyte files, old and future timestamps are valid and are kept.

### 6.4 Duplicates

Within one volume's snapshot, records that reach the same canonical path are duplicates: the record whose batch has the
later captured_utc wins, ties go to the greater record id, the others are DUPLICATE_PATH.

### 6.5 Store

store_bytes counts content once: for each distinct sha256 among the manifest entries (taking the bytes of the first
entry with that digest in manifest order) that is not already present in the prior store index, add bytes rounded up to
a multiple of store.block_size. Prior store index partitions: store_index_prior_a.csv, _b.jsonl, _c.parquet, _d.db; per
digest (lower case) the highest rev decides and only status present counts as stored.

### 6.6 Hard links

Entries of one volume that share a file_id with nlink greater than 1 are one file: the entry with the smallest canonical
path (code-point order) stays and the others are HARDLINK_ALIAS. ARC01 records take file_id and nlink from the row of
arc01_ltfs_index.db with the same canonical path.

## 7. Retention

### 7.1 Classes

Class ages come from Table 1 of retention-schedule-2026-r2.docx (ratified). age_days = floor((window.end - mtime) / 86400).

### 7.2 Extension families

The extension of a name is the longest suffix after a dot that has an effective family (so tar.gz beats gz); a name
that starts with a dot and has no other dot has none. Tables in precedence order: A ext_families_a.tsv, B
ext_families_b.xlsx, C Annex C of retention-schedule-2026-r2.docx, D ext_families_wiki.html, E the table built into
the export set's own tool.zip (member estatectl/ext_families_builtin.tsv; the copy beside the running tool is not
consulted). Within a table the highest rev per extension decides; a retired row is skipped; family N/A defers to the next
table. Annex F corrections of retention-schedule-2026-r3.docx apply as that document states. Extensions compare in lower
case without a leading dot.

### 7.3 Rules

The ratified ruleset is retention_rules_v3.yaml: its active rules in ascending seq, minus rules withdrawn by an approved
withdrawal in retention_rules_v4.toml (v4's own rules are not in force), followed by retention_overlays.toml in ascending
seq. Every rule whose scope is * or the entry's volume is tested and the LAST matching rule decides the class; no match
means class standard. Patterns: family:<CODE> matches the extension family. A pattern ending in / matches directories:
it matches an entry when one of the entry's ancestor directories matches. A pattern containing / (other than a trailing
one) is anchored at the volume root and matched against the whole canonical path; otherwise it is matched against the
basename (or, for directory patterns, against each ancestor directory name). * matches within a segment, ? one character
of a segment, ** any number of segments. On case-insensitive volumes patterns are lowered before matching.

### 7.4 Actions

An entry is PURGED when purge.enabled is true, its class has purge_after_days, age_days is strictly greater than it, no
active legal hold covers it, its purge_exempt subtree flag is not true, and the volume allows purges (see the footnotes
of the schedule). Otherwise action is archive when the class has archive_after_days, age_days is at least that value and
the archive_exempt flag is not true; else keep. For a flag, the subtree key with the longest prefix that covers the path
(whole segments) decides.

### 7.5 Legal holds

Hold registers: legal_holds_register.csv (A), holds_matter_index.xlsx (B), holds_portal_export.html (D) and
legal_hold_windows.ics (E: STATUS CONFIRMED is active, CANCELLED is not; no DTEND means open-ended; X-HOLD-PREFIX is the
prefix). A hold is active when its status is active, its matter (matters sheet of holds_matter_index.xlsx) is not closed,
it has no effective release (legal-hold-memo-q3.docx), from_utc < window.end, and to_utc is empty or > window.start. It
covers paths of its volume equal to or below its prefix (whole segments).

## 8. Journals

Events with timestamp t in [snapshot, window.end) are applied, earlier events are STALE_EVENT and later ones
DEFERRED_EVENT. For journals that record a host (journal_eng01_q3.log, journal_fin01_q3.log, journal_ops01_q3.log) t is
first corrected by the adopted clock incidents. Events apply per volume in ascending sequence number (OBJ01 sequencers
are hexadecimal numbers compared by value), never in timestamp order. CREATE makes a live entry (mtime = t; a live entry
already at that path becomes REPLACED). MODIFY updates bytes, digest and mtime = t of a live entry. RENAME moves a live
entry to the new path, keeping its mtime and origin; an entry already at the new path becomes REPLACED. DELETE removes a
live entry (its origin becomes DELETED). RESTORE brings back the most recently deleted entry of that path. Events whose
path is not live (or, for RESTORE, has nothing deleted) are ORPHAN_EVENT. S3 ObjectCreated events are CREATE;
ObjectRemoved events are DELETE.

## 9. Exceptions

Each record that does not end as the origin of a manifest entry, and is not an applied journal event, is reported once
with the first applicable reason in this order: OUT_OF_AUTHORITY, SUPERSEDED_SCOPE, VOIDED, NOT_A_FILE, STALE_EVENT,
DEFERRED_EVENT, PATH_UNRESOLVED, SIZE_UNKNOWN, HASH_UNRESOLVED, OWNER_UNRESOLVED, DUPLICATE_PATH, ORPHAN_EVENT, DELETED,
REPLACED, HARDLINK_ALIAS, PURGED. Size, digest and owner checks apply to inventory records and to CREATE (all three) and
MODIFY (size and digest) events. A RENAME whose new path does not resolve to the same volume is PATH_UNRESOLVED.

## 10. Catalog continuity and ledger

### 10.1 Entry ids

estate_manifest_q2.parquet is the previous period's manifest. It supplies entry ids and the opening balance only; its
digests, sizes and owners are last period's values. An entry keeps the id of the prior row with status live, a valid
volume code and exactly the same volume and canonical path. Other entries get new ids counting up from the
next_entry_id metadata value, in ascending (volume, path) code-point order.

### 10.2 Ledger

opening is the prior manifest metadata (closing_entries, closing_logical_bytes, closing_store_bytes). movement.carried
counts entries that kept an id, movement.new the others, movement.retired the prior live keys that are not entries now;
opening.entries - retired + new = closing.entries. closing.store_bytes = opening.store_bytes + store bytes of this
period (6.5); closing.id_hash_total = sum of entry ids modulo 1000000007. volumes lists entries and logical_bytes per
volume; exceptions counts exceptions per reason.

### 10.3 Profiles

prod and dev differ only through their effective configuration.

### 10.4 Control records

Every file carries a control record (ctl_id, prev, rows); locations are listed in Appendix S of source-register.md.
prev names the ctl_id of the file before it; the first file's prev is the closing_ctl_id of the prior manifest. rows
counts the file's data records (table rows, list items, events, messages); header, layout, schema and checkpoint lines
are not rows. The chain must visit all 100 files; a chain that breaks means an input is missing.

## 11. Output contract

Outputs are UTF-8 JSON. Entry lists are sorted by (volume, path) in code-point order; exception lists by rec_id.
Digests (sha256) are written in lower-case hex.

profile_<profile>.json: {"profile": p, "config": {flat effective configuration}, "control": {"entries": int,
"logical_bytes": int, "store_bytes": int, "id_hash_total": int}, "entries": [{"entry_id": int, "volume": str,
"path": str, "sha256": str, "bytes": int, "mtime": str, "owner": str, "class": str, "action": "keep" or "archive",
"origin": str}]}. control equals the ledger closing of that profile.

validation_report.json: {"prod": [{"rec_id": str, "reason": str}], "dev": [...]}.

summary.json: {"opening_ctl_id": str, "chain": [{"file": str, "ctl_id": str, "rows": int}] in chain order, "profiles":
{"prod": ledger, "dev": ledger}} where ledger = {"opening": {"entries", "logical_bytes", "store_bytes"}, "movement":
{"carried", "new", "retired"}, "closing": {"entries", "logical_bytes", "store_bytes", "id_hash_total"}, "volumes":
[{"volume", "entries", "logical_bytes"}] sorted by volume, "exceptions": {reason: count}}.

config.json equals the config object of profile_prod.json.
